## 1. What the archive pages actually supply

**Cote 54.** The inspected manuscript concerns the universal vector
extension of an abelian variety, its de Rham realization, Cartier duality,
and the restricted Lie structure in characteristic \(p\). In the official
PDF, scan 003 (manuscript page 2) displays the Hodge exact sequence and
the vector extension; scan 005 (manuscript page 4) discusses the Cartier
dual construction; scan 007 (manuscript page 6) treats Frobenius,
Verschiebung, and the restricted operation on the vector kernel. The
zero restricted operation there belongs to that vector kernel. It is
not a statement that every invariant derivation of an abelian variety
has zero \(p\)-th power. AM-K013 and AM-K017 compute precisely the
different restricted powers that occur on the actual Jacobian.

**Cote 4.** Scan 003 (printed page 2), §8.1.1, relates crystalline and
de Rham cohomology through the stated derived comparison; §8.1.2 records
the associated Tor issue. Scan 005 (printed page 4), §8.4.1–2, singles out
the Frobenius-bijective stable part. The manuscript's terminology
“semi-simple” in this passage refers to that Frobenius-stable part; it
must not be substituted for ordinary linear diagonalizability. Scan 006
(printed page 5), Lemma 8.4.3, compares the stable parts of de Rham and
coherent cohomology and uses the Frobenius action on the filtration.
The subsequent §8.5 contains conditional ingredients. Scan 012 (printed
page 11), §8.6, asks a monodromy question for the indicated finite-field
local systems and explains a possible trace/congruence consequence.
It is a proposed route in the manuscript, not a theorem about all
number-field Hecke \(L\)-functions.

These are the exact archive loci used here. Their content organizes
the realization and monodromy questions; no newly computed theta slope
or automorphic nonvanishing is inferred from an archival question.

## 2. Formal groups, crystals, and integral differential operators

Over a finite extension where the Jacobian has good reduction, its
\(p\)-divisible group and Cartier dual determine dual Dieudonné
objects. The accepted DP_MULT DP30–34 and DP49 distinguish the
one-dimensional height-three factor from its two-dimensional dual.
The three labelled tangent directions are not three independent
height-three formal lines. Frobenius permutes the CM labels and
Verschiebung controls the restricted derivations. This is why the
restricted nilpotence exponents in AM-K013 are direction dependent.

The exact finite-level framework is SGA 3, Exposé VII_A, §§6.2–6.4,
7.2–7.4, and 8.1, as carried in DP_MULT. For the de Rham/finite-group
comparison, the carried primary locators are Oda (1969), Theorem 3.19,
p. 89, and Corollary 5.11, p. 131. DP_MULT's source audit additionally
fixes Tate (1967), §2.2, Proposition 1, p. 162, equation (3), p. 163,
equation (4), p. 164, and §2.3, Proposition 3, p. 166; Zink's dual
display operators are Definition 19, equations (22)–(24), pp. 143–144,
and the formal-biextension injection is Proposition 121 and equation
(243), pp. 241 and 246. Those established results are cited, not proved
again in this run.

The new theta statements concern the completed local rings and actual
sections of a polarized abelian scheme. In AM-K012 the explicit ordered
divisor chart is étale on a dense open and supplies a ring stable under
the invariant derivations. AM-K017 extends a logarithmic Hessian across
the theta divisor by checking every horizontal and vertical divisor and
using normality. It then uses properness to turn a global regular
function on the special fibre into a constant. This is a scheme-theoretic
argument on the actual integral model. It does not replace a missing
integral reduced theta frame by a complex analytic one.

The resulting Hurwitz series use the ordinary invariant operators
(D_i^n), with factorials in the multiplication law. Their reductions
and restricted-power identities are explicit. A Chern connection on an
ample theta line is not thereby made into a global flat algebraic
connection or a Dieudonné crystal. To make that additional assertion
one would have to construct the relevant stratification, integrable
connection, and Frobenius structure. The present proof does not do so.

For the oscillator, the same distinction has an exact EGA locator:
EGA IV_4, Theorem 16.11.2, p. 54, and Remarks 16.11.3(ii), p. 55.
Over an integral base the full sheaf of differential operators contains
divided derivatives and is generally larger than the algebra generated
by coordinates and ordinary derivations. AM-DO-003 constructs an
integral Weyl-algebra module, becoming the familiar delta
\(D\)-module after rationalization. It does not claim an integral
action of the full EGA differential-operator sheaf.

## 3. Picard schemes, the theta line, and finite models

The Jacobian is a Picard variety. The precise carried FGA references
are Bourbaki exposé 232, Theorem 3.1, pp. 148–149; exposé 236,
Corollary 1.7, p. 229, Corollary 2.3, p. 232, and Proposition 2.10(ii),
p. 236. They give the representability and base-change framework used
in DP_MULT DP22 and DP49. The actual divisor and section calculations
in the present run are additional calculations on that Picard scheme.

ALP-LIFT-07 distinguishes the non-effective characteristic (D_{135})
from the invariant trace-one theta characteristic. They differ by a
nonzero element of (J[2]). This is a distinction in the relative
Picard scheme, so an analytic normalization or a scalar change cannot
remove it. Pullback by an odd integer preserves the nontrivial
two-torsion difference. AM-DO-015 instead uses the specified Mumford
translation; AM-R-006 computes its actual scalar.

ALP-LIFT-08 computes the leading determinant section on an integral
Picard chart, including residue characteristic two. Its first
nonzero jet is the determinant of the cup-product matrix,
(u_1u_3-u_2^2). The Hodge-basis transformations in ALP-LIFT-05
then determine the vertical contents at 2 and 7. The objects are
line bundles, their primitive sections, and their integral jet
images over specified discrete valuation rings. An equality after
tensoring with the fraction field would not determine these contents.

AM-R-004–006 and ALP-LIFT-02–06 give the corresponding Betti lattice,
de Rham basis, complex metric, determinant metric, and exact scalar
comparison. Thus the height identities live on the metrized Hodge
determinant line, with the finite integral lattices retained. The
archimedean Cauchy estimate and the finite derivative estimate are
different realizations of the same normalized section; neither may
be changed independently in the cost calculation.

## 4. Biextensions and the phase problem

The polarized Poincaré line on \(J\times J^\vee\) is a
\(\mathbf G_m\)-biextension, rigidified along both zero sections.
Pullbacks, duality, and transposition organize the theta-group and
trace-one constructions. The exact inspected SGA 7 I loci are:

- Exposé VII, §2.7, printed pp. 169–170: transposition of a
  biextension. These are PDF pages 174–175 in the original scan.
- Exposé VII, equation (2.9.1) and Proposition 2.9.3, printed
  pp. 172–174: the forgetful functor to birigidified torsors and
  its fully faithful form under the displayed hypotheses.
  Example 2.9.5, p. 175, gives the abelian/Picard setting.
- Exposé VIII, Theorem 7.1(a–b), printed pp. 299–300: extension
  over a trait. In part (b), the component groups enter a canonical
  obstruction pairing into \(\mathbf Q/\mathbf Z\); under the
  stated bounded-torsion hypothesis its vanishing is equivalent
  to extension. The connected-fibre case is explicitly separated.
- Exposé VIII, Proposition 7.4(b), printed pp. 310–311: full
  faithfulness over a reduced base for the indicated smooth
  group schemes with connected generic fibres; over a normal
  base, with one group having connected fibres, the stated
  generic-fibre criterion describes the essential image.

The local finite Gauss factors of AM-DO-017 are additional data in a
specified analytic/Heisenberg realization. The uniqueness of a
birigidified biextension structure does not say that every chosen
intertwiner of its realizations has scalar one. AM-DO-020 proves
that scalar assertion at relative split places by evaluating positive
normalized lattice indicators. AM-DO-021 exhibits the actual residual
compact sign at the ramified place 7. This is a normalization issue
inside an existing realization, not a counterexample to the SGA 7
existence or uniqueness theorems.

The common period in AM-DO-018 is retained for every moved lattice.
Its coefficient denominators are intersection indices, and its norm
is computed with specified probability Haar measure. In geometric
language the Heisenberg eigenspaces are the one-dimensional section
spaces of principal theta lines on isogenous complex tori, compared
over common finite covers. Their phase and arithmetic integral
structure are separate from the isomorphism class of the line bundle.

## 5. Group schemes, weights, and the oscillator

The exact fresh SGA 3 locator for a diagonalizable-group action is
Exposé I, Proposition 4.7.3; Corollary 4.7.3.1 gives the corresponding
graded-algebra/equivariant-affine-scheme statement. Accordingly the
charges in T-052 are actual characters of a torus, and its degree
pieces are equivariant locally free modules. The nonnegative
equal-charge construction of the accepted T-053 is a specified
graded coordinate subalgebra. It is not merely a dimension count.

AM-DO-002 and AM-DO-007 compare the actual character weights of the
canonical ball representation with the required polynomial degrees.
AM-DO-006 proves an obstruction for the entire creation-operator
weight set. AM-DO-003 changes polarization by a Fourier/Weyl
algebra map and records why the resulting delta module is not the
positive Fock vacuum. AM-DO-005 supplies a selected bicanonical
comparison with an exact local Haar pairing, so the obstruction must
not be generalized to every representation or determinant twist.

AM-R-007 belongs to the reductive-group side: the exact determinant
sequence separates (SU\(V\)) from the norm-one torus in (U\(V\)),
and strong approximation gives representatives for a Hermitian lattice
genus with level structure. SGA 3 supplies the group-scheme framework;
the approximation theorem actually used is Rapinchuk (2013),
Theorem 2.3, p. 281. The source locator is not relabelled as a theorem
of SGA 3. ALP-LIFT-09 then constructs an actual automorphic character
and theta integral on the norm-one torus. That analytic integral
requires its Haar measure and splitting, beyond the algebraic group
scheme and its representation class.

## 6. Lambda rings, K-theory, and what a class does not determine

For a finite locally free representation \(V\), its exterior powers
define the lambda operations on its exact Grothendieck group. The
carried exact locators are SGA 6, Exposé V, §2 (lambda operations),
§3 (gamma operations and filtration), Proposition 4.2, Definition 4.4,
Proposition 4.8, and Corollary 4.9 (the universal representation-ring
description); Exposé VI, Theorem 3.3, is the associated Schur-functor
framework used by GCT_PLETH. Cote 133, scan 006, records the archival
pre-lambda/classifying-topos framework, as carried in that run's
source study. The SGA 6 download attempt in this run failed; these
numbered references remain carried references, not fresh primary
inspections.

Each finite degree of the oscillator polynomial algebra has a class
formed by symmetric and Schur operations. A tensor-to-Hom map such
as AM-DO-009 induces the corresponding equality of representation
classes after dualizing the source factor. The map itself retains the
contragredient action, the alternating form and the determinant roots.
The representation class alone forgets the positive metric, the
integral section lattice, finite Weil phases and the actual map on
automorphic forms.

Likewise AM-R-002's content ideal is data of an actual integral
linear map applied to an actual vector. Its least denominator and
dual metric can be expressed through determinant lines and metrized
lattices; they are not recovered from a virtual rank. AM-R-001's
cost subtraction and ALP-LIFT-06's directional cancellation compare
those retained numerical realizations. No lambda-ring identity is
used to erase a height, a covolume or an endpoint denominator.

## 7. CM motives, Artin motives, and the RH boundary

AM-R-003 uses the primitive CM type to separate the simple rational
weight-one Hodge structure of (J) from an elliptic induced type.
The corresponding CM realizations are those of (h^1(J)), with
its endomorphism action and polarization. Products and field
extensions do not turn that simple isotypy class into the source's
elliptic Serre-tensor class. AM-DO-010 instead identifies specified
CM eigensummands with actual Galois twists up to isogeny. It does
not identify their integral models without the separate lattice data.

For AM-K001–004 the order-(r) Kummer character is a rank-one
Artin representation over the cyclotomic base. Over the open arithmetic
base excluding its ramified primes it is a lisse rank-one sheaf with
finite monodromy. Its induced representation is the stated augmentation
representation over \(\mathbf Q\). The exact representation identity
therefore carries the ramified Euler factors as recorded in the proof.
For (r=3), the explicit isomorphism of two CM elliptic curves over
the Kummer extension identifies the character with a line in their
Hom realization. This gives an actual descent cocycle, rather than
an analogy based on dimension.

The proper/smooth realization framework is SGA 4, Exposé XII,
Theorems 5.1–5.2; Exposé XV, Theorem 2.1; Exposé XVI, Theorem 2.1,
and SGA 5, Exposé VI, §§1.2.1, 1.4.2–1.4.3, pp. 265–266, as
carried in CX_GRH/GRH_NIGHT. For finite-field weights the exact carried
locators are Deligne's *Weil I*, Theorem 1.6, p. 276, and *Weil II*,
Corollary 3.3.9, p. 207. They control Frobenius eigenvalues in their
stated finite-field cohomological settings. In particular an Artin
line has weight zero. These statements do not put the zeros of its
number-field Hecke \(L\)-function on the critical line.

AM-K005 isolates the remaining analytic implication through an
explicit every-epsilon arithmetic window. AM-K007–011 supply ray
characters, splitting twists and CM hosts while retaining ramified
conductors, prime norms and host costs. Cote 4 §8.6's monodromy
question is relevant background for such realization questions; it
does not supply the missing arithmetic window. The accepted
Dirichlet theorem of Alpöge remains an input with its original scope.
No statement of Masullo's draft is used as an unconditional theorem.

## 10. Additional checked pairing and twist refinements

AM-DO-024 supplies a positive comparison between the actual finite Weil translate and the direct Heisenberg average on the E-prime host, together with the exact probability-Haar pairing and adjoints. These are identities of metrized section lines in the biextension/Heisenberg realization of section4, stronger data than the isomorphism class of a Picard line. They do not determine an automorphic quotient mass. AM-R-008 supplies actual integral diagonal maps for tensoring compatible coefficient modules by a unitary determinant character, within the SGA3 character and SGA6 representation K-theory frameworks of sections5–6. Its all-prime content preservation comes from root-of-unity scalars, not from equality in K0. The first-lift target-level membership and its refinement cost remain additional obligations. AM-DO-023 is held pending reconciliation of the general character convention; its unchanged-character local vanishing has a separate narrower scope.

## 11. Unchanged first raw kernel and its mass

AM-DO-036 proves positivity for the actual unchanged first raw theta tensor on the principally polarized J14/J56 hosts. Its finite lattice realization is the section/Heisenberg construction organized by the rigidified Poincare biextension of SGA7 I, VII2.7/2.9 (pp169–174) and VIII7.4(b) (pp310–311), and the Picard representability interfaces FGA232 Theorem3.1 (pp148–149) and FGA236 Corollary1.7 (p229),2.3 (p232). Those scheme results provide the object and functoriality, not this analytic positivity. The latter is a convergent doubled theta norm calculation with anisotropic target, using GQT7.1 (p33); its target quotient mass remains an explicit scalar. It therefore removes the first fixed-kernel vanishing gate of DP_MULT Q3e and narrows THETA3 G1, without supplying an integral later image lattice or terminal mass identity. The common CM weight-one motive is retained; its Dieudonne and Cartier-dual realization remains the cote4/cote54 correspondence recorded in sections2–3. No finite-field weight statement or equality in representation K-theory supplies the missing global mass or number-field RH.

## 12. Mixed periods, embedding ideals and local quotient scalars

AM-K035 expresses the polarized Picard variety through its Betti/de Rham comparison with an actual per-embedding period vector. Its coordinate ideals are integral module intersections over Spec(O_M), and its reduced theta jets are formal sections of the rigidified line in the Picard/biextension setting of FGA232 Theorem3.1 (pp148–149), FGA236 Corollary1.7 (p229),2.3 (p232), SGA7 I VII2.7/2.9 (pp169–174) and VIII7.4(b) (pp310–311). The all-order algebraic formal coordinates use the EGA IV4 16.11.2–16.11.3(ii) interface (pp54–55) already recorded above. The explicit analytic period and unit-Fock constants are new comparisons, not consequences of representability alone. At11 its cD is a unit, so it preserves the previously proved Dieudonne/Cartier-dual selected-jet content; cote4 and cote54 scans5–7 remain the exact historical framework locators of sections2–3.

ALP-LIFT-24 and AM-DO-037 calculate actual local quotient maps and their Haar/L/Gauss scalars. Their finite inputs are section representations of the same Heisenberg extension; the literal DegOne condition is the statement e=f=1 for the relevant finite localization. An identity in the representation lambda-ring or K-group (SGA6 VI3.3) records types but does not determine these periods, integral content or the global terminal mass. Likewise finite-field weights of the CM motive do not supply number-field RH for the Kummer character. These scope distinctions carry forward DP_MULT Q3e and THETA3 G1 without changing CX_GRH CX-ALG-024.

## 13. Fixed-edge descent and compact contractions

K036 is a descent statement for complete formal sections and their torsion translations on the fixed Picard/biextension family. It uses the exact algebraic rational-isometry scalar and finite-class reduction, not representability alone; its FGA/SGA7/EGA locators are those in sections4 and12. The explicit nc measures a finite isogeny/Heisenberg induction coefficient, while Cartier/Dieudonne slopes (cote4; cote54 scans5–7) concern the separate formal section content. LIFT25 computes an actual contraction in the compact representation category and its Fock metric, refining the SGA3 I4.7.3/4.7.3.1 and SGA6 VI3.3 class information. The dimension denominator and invariant-tensor norm are distinct. No virtual representation or motivic equality erases these scalar costs.

## 14. Integral branching with its metric

R019 makes the SGA3/SGA6 representation/flag restriction of section13 quantitative: the actual integral minor vector, spectral integer and metrized branch have exact factorial expressions. SGA3 I4.7.3/4.7.3.1 and SGA6 VI3.3 organize the representations, while Molev Proposition2.4 supplies the classical contravariant norm input and the new path calculation fixes its source normalization. The integer scalar is not an integral splitting theorem for every chosen lattice. The Picard/biextension and cote4/cote54 formal realizations remain those already identified; no new motivic or number-field RH consequence follows from this metric refinement.

## 15. ALP-LIFT-26: fixed coupled analytic image

The two selected correspondences have a nonzero composition on their actual complex automorphic realization, with prescribed local sections and every multiplicity copy retained. In the Picard/biextension interpretation of sections12–14, the tensor identity uses the same transported line-bundle frames; its character and volume factors are part of the realization, not an identification of integral lattices. The exact Grothendieck locators remain FGA232 Theorem3.1 pp148–149 and236 Corollary1.7 p229/2.3 p232 for Picard geometry, SGA7I VII2.7/2.9 pp169–174 and VIII7.4(b) pp310–311 for biextensions, and EGAIV4 16.11.2–3(ii) pp54–55 for jets. None of these citations identifies the image of the arithmetic class map or its terminal branching projection. In lambda/K-theory the selected Schur factors are the same factors already recorded in section14; in motivic or ell-adic language no new algebraic correspondence comparison or RH statement follows from this analytic nonvanishing theorem.

## 16. AM-K037: integral polynomial functor and class-map realization

The map CR7 is a morphism of the specified integral Weyl polynomial functors over Z; the monic-determinant descent proves it extends from GL to the full matrix scheme on the relevant image. Its evaluation against the independently fixed algebraic theta jet is the actual arithmetic class map. This places the new fact directly in the polynomial-functor/lambda-ring language of the earlier SGA6 VI3.3 comparison, while its jet realization uses EGAIV4 16.11.2–3(ii), pp54–55. The fixed line bundle and reduced theta section retain the Picard and biextension locators of sections12–15 (FGA232/236 and SGA7I VII–VIII). Coevaluation is the usual duality morphism for the selected coefficient representation; its norm and finite stabilizers are part of the realization and are not removed by the K-theory description. No new identification of an integral motivic image, ell-adic weights or Kummer RH is claimed.

## 17. AM-DO-038: terminal contraction and mass

The fixed branch is a morphism between the selected coefficient realizations; the finite class pairing contracts it with the dual row and weights each class by its actual finite stabilizer. The top automorphic realization realizes this same pairing and introduces the exact adelic level volume. This is a comparison at the complex/arithmetic coefficient level, not a new identity of schemes or motives. Its polynomial/jet part has the same SGA6 VI3.3 and EGAIV4 16.11.2–3(ii) pp54–55 locators; its fixed line/frame transports retain FGA232 Theorem3.1 pp148–149, FGA236 Corollary1.7 p229 and2.3 p232, and SGA7I VII2.7/2.9 pp169–174 andVIII7.4(b) pp310–311. Stabilizer cancellation and compact Schur integration do not imply nonzero algebraic cycle classes or ell-adic purity. The latter realizations require the independently stated correspondence comparison, still open in THETA3 G1.

## 18. AM-R-020: integral Weyl-image sandwich

The new inclusions are statements about finite integral representation modules over Spec(Z) and their localizations, and they survive extension to Spec(O_M). They refine the rational restriction/Schur-functor information organized by SGA3I4.7.3/4.7.3.1 and SGA6VI3.3: equality of rational representation classes does not determine an integral image or its elementary divisors. The exact finite-place bounds use the specified source divided-power lattice and normalized duality. Their application to the Picard/biextension theta section retains the existing FGA232 Theorem3.1 pp148–149, FGA236 Corollary1.7 p229/2.3 p232, SGA7I VII2.7/2.9 pp169–174 andVIII7.4(b) pp310–311 locators; R020 adds no claim about that earlier image. The Dieudonne/Cartier-dual framework remains cote4 and cote54 scans5–7 as recorded in sections2–3. No new motivic correspondence, ell-adic weight or Kummer RH implication is asserted.

## 19. AM-K038 and ALP-LIFT-27: coupled section descent and faithful analytic image

K038 is an explicit descent construction of the two composed theta-section differential operators on the finite decorated lattice quotient, with coefficient modules over O_M and actual stabilizer weights. Its polynomial scheme maps use the existing SGA3I4.7.3/4.7.3.1, SGA6VI3.3 and EGAIV4 16.11.2–3(ii) pp54–55 interfaces. The section/translation geometry retains FGA232 Theorem3.1 pp148–149, FGA236 Corollary1.7 p229/2.3 p232, SGA7I VII2.7/2.9 pp169–174 andVIII7.4(b) pp310–311. The strict/projective refinement is an actual quotient mass identity, not a change in the underlying Picard or motivic object. LIFT27 proves faithfulness of the specified full analytic image on its initial ramified space; it does not identify a faithful algebraic correspondence after terminal restriction. The formal selected content remains within the cote4/cote54 Cartier–Dieudonne framework already recorded. Neither analytic injectivity nor equality of lambda-ring classes proves a nonzero terminal motive, an ell-adic weight assertion or Kummer RH.

## 20. AM-R-021: character twists and numerical weight windows

Tensoring by a determinant character shifts both u and v equally; the v-width and first u−v gap are invariant. The full arithmetic budget requires an inequality between these invariants, violated by the current equally spaced family. This is a numerical restriction on coefficient representations within the SGA3I4.7.3/4.7.3.1 and SGA6VI3.3 framework, not a new geometric obstruction to all theta correspondences. It neither changes the existing Picard/biextension/formal crystal constructions nor supplies an ell-adic or motivic RH conclusion. The exact source and prior-run locators are R021§1–3.

## 21. AM-DO-039: finite integral tests and relative representation models

The common split-line kernel is a kernel of morphisms of the specified finite polynomial representation modules over Z and their localizations. Its characteristic-zero dimension is not determined by a single highest line. This refines the coefficient-restriction picture of SGA3I4.7.3/4.7.3.1 and SGA6VI3.3; it does not assert a kernel in the actual theta-correspondence image. The local Q open/closed orbits are relative representation models of one fixed analytic vector and are not different motivic realizations. The Picard, biextension and jet comparisons retain FGA232 Theorem3.1 pp148–149, FGA236 Corollary1.7 p229/2.3 p232, SGA7I VII2.7/2.9 pp169–174 andVIII7.4(b) pp310–311, and EGAIV4 16.11.2–3(ii) pp54–55. No new cycle nonvanishing, ell-adic weight assertion or L-function theorem follows.

## 22. AM-K039: actual section integrality and class descent

The new divisibility theorem is about the integral local-section module of the fixed principal theta line and its actual class descent over a DVR above11. Prime-to11 Heisenberg translation is identified through the normalized torsion difference connection, so the reduced exponential is retained. Its Picard/biextension/jet setting has the exact FGA232 Theorem3.1 pp148–149, FGA236 Corollary1.7 p229/2.3 p232, SGA7I VII2.7/2.9 pp169–174 andVIII7.4(b) pp310–311, EGAIV4 16.11.2–3(ii) pp54–55 locators already recorded. The selected differential functor acts on integral Weyl modules over Z (SGA3I4.7.3/4.7.3.1; SGA6VI3.3), while the numerical11 bound uses the established Cartier–Dieudonne comparison (cote4; cote54 scans5–7). Finite class stabilizers are explicit descent denominators; a K-theory class or a motivic description does not remove them. These inclusions give no nonzero terminal cycle, ell-adic weight theorem or Kummer RH.

## 23. AM-R-022: a feasible coefficient-weight cloud

The new profile supplies explicit highest weights and binary interlacing grids in the same integral representation framework (SGA3I4.7.3/4.7.3.1; SGA6VI3.3), with exact real Scale parameters and rational coefficient weights. It changes the coefficient representations used by the jets, not the underlying J-power, Picard scheme or biextension line; the exact FGA/SGA7/EGA jet locators are those in R022§5 and sections12–22 above. Numerical window feasibility does not identify the integral theta image or a motivic correspondence and implies no ell-adic weight or RH assertion.

## 24. ALP-LIFT-28: coherent jets and a bottom detector

The new bottom detector is an application of coherent finite-jet interpolation on a projective minimal compactification. Its exact Grothendieck input is EGAIII.1 Theorem2.2.1(ii), p100: sufficiently ample twists kill higher cohomology of the coherent kernel of finite-jet restriction. The finite jet space retains EGAIV4 16.11.2–3(ii), pp54–55. Mumford1977 Theorem3.1 and Proposition3.4(a),(b) supply the canonical extension and ample logarithmic-canonical line. These results construct an actual analytic/coherent detector at the explicit level, with stabilizer-equivariant coefficients; they do not identify it with a prescribed theta correspondence or bound its norm. The Picard/biextension locators remain FGA232/236 and SGA7I VII–VIII as above. No ell-adic cycle or motivic L-function conclusion follows.

## 25. AM-K040: coherent CM character types and arithmetic coefficient fields

The principal/base-ideal compatibility builds actual algebraic angular characters for every weight in a fixed lattice, with one finite value field and preserved finite conductors. In the CM-motive language this is the infinity-type/finite-realization datum of rank-one character realizations; it is not a proof of RH or an automatic comparison with the mixed theta motive. The underlying CM Picard scheme and biextension line retain FGA232 Theorem3.1 pp148–149, FGA236 Corollary1.7 p229/2.3 p232 and SGA7I VII2.7/2.9 pp169–174, VIII7.4(b) pp310–311. The new theorem is an ideal/character construction, not a new theorem from those geometric locators. Its finite radical-field bound controls degree only; no unrecorded discriminant or height bound is supplied.

## 26. AM-DO-040: certified prime and decorated lattice data

The explicit algebraic integers piT give finite etale split residue fibres and the actual decorated integral lattice support for the replacement coefficient weights. Their deterministic primality, norm and stabilizer assertions are arithmetic inputs to the existing scheme/jet comparison, not new theorems about Picard schemes. The exact geometric locators remain FGA232/236, SGA7I VII–VIII and EGAIV4 16.11.2–3(ii) stated above. Strict/projective level masses and finite character-field enlargements remain numerical costs; the lambda-ring or motivic description does not cancel them. No new nonvanishing or L-function conclusion follows.

## 27. ALP-LIFT-29: translated finite packet and the same coherent jets

The finite level and translation define an actual collection of special points with exactly the original isotropy groups; the displayed local matrix calculation fixes the intersection of their integral models at T. The coherent detector therefore uses the same EGAIII.1 Theorem2.2.1(ii), p100, and EGAIV4 16.11.2–3(ii), pp54–55, finite-jet interfaces as LIFT28. The translation is an isometry of the complex automorphic realization; it is not asserted to preserve an integral section lattice or have zero arithmetic height. The Picard/biextension locators remain FGA232 Theorem3.1 pp148–149, FGA236 Corollary1.7 p229/2.3 p232 and SGA7I VII2.7/2.9 pp169–174, VIII7.4(b) pp310–311. No identification of the prescribed correspondence, motive, ell-adic cycle or L-function follows from the packet comparison alone.

The fixed-field obstruction is a finite Galois quotient statement over Spec(Q), independent of the valid mixed Picard/section construction. The conditional rank bounds concern the size of a full CM realization of h1(J^N); they do not remove R003's separate simple-isogeny/type obstruction. The exact FGA/SGA7 Picard and biextension locators, SGA6VI3.3 representation framework and cote4/cote54 crystal realizations are retained in R023§5. No change in a virtual representation, motive or ell-adic realization relaxes the displayed integer and Scale inequalities. No L-function theorem is obtained.

## 29. AM-K041: actual new-profile integral image

The all-word bound is an inclusion of the actual finite-class coherent Weyl coefficient modules over the DVR above11, with both stabilizer denominators. The primitive theta section is transported through the normalized torsion/Poincare connection on its formal Picard completion, retaining the Chern exponential. Schur operations retain SGA6VI3.3; the exact Picard/biextension and jet locators are FGA232 Theorem3.1 pp148–149, FGA236 Corollary1.7 p229/2.3 p232, SGA7I VII2.7/2.9 pp169–174 andVIII7.4(b) pp310–311, EGAIV4 16.11.2–3(ii) pp54–55. The separate unrestricted analytic injectivity does not imply a nonzero restricted motivic or ell-adic correspondence. No Kummer RH result follows.

## 30. AM-R-024 and ALP-LIFT-30: terminal integral rows and negative-line transport

R024 gives inclusions of the actual image in Weyl/dual-Weyl coefficient lattices over the DVR above11, retaining normalized pairing, finite stabilizers and every EqFun constraint. The exact SGA3I4.7.3/4.7.3.1 and SGA6VI3.3 frameworks do not remove these indices. No algebraic-cycle nonvanishing, ell-adic weight or Kummer RH statement is inferred.

R024.18–21 applies R002’s metrized image-line balance to the actual class-independent branching scalar. Its principal finite divisor and all-embedding real logarithmic norm agree, so the corresponding scalar has degree zero. This is the product formula on the number-field base, not a cancellation of the remaining integral correspondence, restricted image, or detector norm.

## 31. AM-K042: neat quotients and bounded coherent interpolation
